# Proposed OEIS comments: A240456 to A250815

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A240456

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A240456>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [1614](../papers/01501-02000/01614-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 8*a(n-1) -20*a(n-2) +34*a(n-3) -84*a(n-4) +92*a(n-5) -68*a(n-6) +222*a(n-7) +13*a(n-8) -251*a(n-9) +25*a(n-10) -495*a(n-11) +485*a(n-12) -44*a(n-13) -180*a(n-14) +554*a(n-15) -648*a(n-16) +14*a(n-17) +152*a(n-18) -190*a(n-19) -140*a(n-20) +397*a(n-21) -273*a(n-22) +75*a(n-23) +167*a(n-24) -136*a(n-25) +27*a(n-26)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 36 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 25. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A240478

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A240478>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [9875](../papers/09501-10000/09875-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) + a(n-2) - 2*a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(2 + 2*x - x^2 - 2*x^3) / (1 - 3*x - x^2 + 2*x^4). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 24 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Every cell the condition names lies a bounded number of lines away -- two of them, where it reaches a knight's move -- so a window of that width decides the condition on the line in the middle, and the lines with nothing far enough beyond them are decided at the end. Counting a line at a time therefore gives a transfer matrix with 4 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A240558

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A240558>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [11140](../papers/11001-11500/11140-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: -(n+2)*(n^2-5)*a(n) +8*(-2*n-1)*a(n-1) +16*(n-1)*(n^2+2*n-4)*a(n-2)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 14 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 0, so the recurrence holds for n > 0. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A240787

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A240787>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10375](../papers/10001-10500/10375-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 94 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 788 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 94, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A242429

What the entry records as unsettled:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

```

## A242543

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A242543>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3825](../papers/03501-04000/03825-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) +4*a(n-3) +4*a(n-4).
> Empirical: G.f.: -2*x*(6+4*x+12*x^2+9*x^3) / ( (2*x^2+2*x-1)*(2*x^2+1) ). - _R. J. Mathar_, May 18 2014

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 36 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A243585

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A243585>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [10214](../papers/10001-10500/10214-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence: 2*n*(2*n-1)*(3*n-5)*a(n) +(-123*n^3+328*n^2-249*n+60)*a(n-1) +50*(n-1)*(2*n-3)*(3*n-2)*a(n-2)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 14 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the closed form already on the entry. It is a sum of finitely many hypergeometric terms, so every shift quotient is a rational function of n and the recurrence collapses to one rational identity in n, which is true. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A243635>  
Result obtained: **8 September 2026**  
Paper: [9169](../papers/09001-09500/09169-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 7*a(n-1) - 14*a(n-2) + 21*a(n-4) - 7*a(n-5) - 6*a(n-6).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(4 - 19*x + 14*x^2 + 30*x^3 - 20*x^4 - 12*x^5) / ((1 - x)*(1 + x)*(1 - 2*x)*(1 - 3*x)*(1 - 2*x - x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Nov 02 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 55 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 0. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A243760

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A243760>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [10228](../papers/10001-10500/10228-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence: 245*n*(n-1)*(n+1)*a(n) -140*n*(n-1)*(28*n-53)*a(n-1) +4*(n-1)*(5812*n^2-29864*n+36585)*a(n-2) +16*(-3368*n^3+35040*n^2-111784*n+110667)*a(n-3) +128*(-152*n^3-1224*n^2+14756*n-29655)*a(n-4) +2048*(2*n-9)*(74*n^2-567*n+988)*a(n-5) -98304*(n-6)*(2*n-9)*(2*n-11)*a(n-6)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 07 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 5, so the recurrence holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A244886

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A244886>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [10869](../papers/10501-11000/10869-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence: n*a(n) +(-5*n+3)*a(n-1) +2*(n)*a(n-2) +(13*n-30)*a(n-3) +3*(-1)*a(n-4) +(-8*n+21)*a(n-5) +3*(-n+3)*a(n-6)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jan 24 2020

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 4, so the recurrence holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A245088

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A245088>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [11229](../papers/11001-11500/11229-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: All the terms are odd integers. Moreover, a(p) == 3 (mod p) for any prime p.
> In 2015 Guo, Mao and Pan confirmed the conjecture partially by proving that n*a(n) is always an integer. - _Zhi-Wei Sun_, Nov 14 2015
> Conjecture: +n^2*(23656*n-2233)*(n-1)*a(n) +(n-1)*(23656*n^3+655645*n^2-2273556*n+1915600)*a(n-1) -2*(n-2) *(177048*n^3-3748011*n^2+13870329*n-15407140)*a(n-2) -2*(n-3) *(2020728*n^3-18138829*n^2+63885382*n-73802216)*a(n-3) -5*(n-4) *(782552*n^3-11993599*n^2+55520403*n-80051816)*a(n-4) +125*(66088*n-203507)*(n-5)*(n-4)^2*a(n-5)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jul 12 2014

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A247170

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A247170>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [10890](../papers/10501-11000/10890-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectured: +n*(2*n-7)*a(n) +(n-1)*(2*n-9)*a(n-1) +2*(-2*n^2+9*n-6)*a(n-2) +2*(-6*n^2+33*n-38)*a(n-3) +3*(-2*n^2+15*n-26)*a(n-4) +(2*n-5)*(n-5)*a(n-5) = 0.

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 4, so the recurrence holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A248434

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A248434>  
Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [11219](../papers/11001-11500/11219-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) -2*a(n-3) +a(n-4).
> Empirical for n mod 2 = 0: a(n) = (3/2)*n^2 + n + 1.
> Empirical for n mod 2 = 1: a(n) = (3/2)*n^2 + n - (1/2).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the closed form already on the entry. It is a sum of finitely many hypergeometric terms, so every shift quotient is a rational function of n and the recurrence collapses to one rational identity in n, which is true. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Paper: [9223](../papers/09001-09500/09223-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) + 5*a(n-2) + 2*a(n-3) - 16*a(n-4) - 28*a(n-5) - 8*a(n-6).
> Empirical g.f.: 4*x*(24 + 23*x - 27*x^2 - 147*x^3 - 186*x^4 - 50*x^5) / ((1 - 2*x)*(1 - x - 7*x^2 - 16*x^3 - 16*x^4 - 4*x^5)). - _Colin Barker_, Nov 08 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 25 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 0. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A250514

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A250514>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10810](../papers/10501-11000/10810-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 50 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 52 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 50, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A250654

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A250654>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3715](../papers/03501-04000/03715-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) - 2*a(n-2); also a(n) = 2^(n-1)*36 + (5*2^(n-1)-1)*6 + 2^(n+1).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(32 - 29*x) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 2*x)). - _Colin Barker_, Nov 15 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 128 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026

## A250679

Paper: [10759](../papers/10501-11000/10759-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 58 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 96 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 58, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A250737

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A250737>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3750](../papers/03501-04000/03750-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) - 2*a(n-2); a(n) = 2^(n+1) + 14.
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(9 - 16*x) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 2*x)). - _Colin Barker_, Nov 16 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 16 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
